GCTS research notebook · 6 November 2026 UTC

Equality search for Wang proofs

The search found 12 checked proofs in arithmetic and Hilbert incidence. It proposes equalities and literal substitutions on demand. The same translation-only Wang palette checks the resulting inference records. Three premise-deletion controls have explicit finite countermodels.

SearchControlsWang squaresRead the proofs

Reuse equality explanations during search

Build a bounded term setSeed it with ground subterms from the formulas. For addition, apply the supplied recurrence equations to propose related terms.
Merge equal termsKeep an explanation for each merge. Congruence merges function applications when their arguments already agree.
Produce the refutationUse explanations to transport facts across equal terms. Keep the dependency path to the contradiction and check it against the source.

For the incidence transfer, two named distinct points lie on both lines. The symbolic incidence axiom gives equality of the lines. Substitution then transfers the third point’s incidence. The search sees the typed symbols and axioms.

For addition, the term proposer follows the two given recursion equations. For each of the three fixed successor counts it derives the identity for every value of the first argument.

The largest addition request used 55 proposed terms. Exhaustive term generation reached the 64-term bound. The certificate checks every resulting equality step against the supplied recursion equations.

Congruence closure and proof-producing explanations are established techniques. This experiment connects them to the existing Wang proof records. Nelson and Oppen · Nieuwenhuis and Oliveras

Compare the search arms

The eager control grounds equality axioms before watched unit propagation. Both internal arms use the same finite term domains. The larger eager control allows ten times as many instances and grounding attempts. Z3 4.15.4 checks the original formulas with uninterpreted sorts and native equality, then independently checks the same bounded ordinary clauses.

Pinned Z3 release · Z3 equality guide

See the Wang transitions for a proof line

Each displayed square comes from the final five verifier transitions for the selected proof line. Edge colors represent exact codes, and the outlined square carries the tape head. Hover for codes.

Read the checked derivations

All 12 derivations have compact records checked from their original formulas. Three also have full Wang transition witnesses. Grounding and resolution were produced by the search.

A separate command-line test proved the six-successor addition identity outside the comparison suite. The independent checker accepted its 138 proof lines. The command also returned a bounded result on the Hilbert premise-deletion control.
